## NCT07631026
Wearable Device Tracked Recovery From Autologous Breast Reconstructive Surgery

Intervention: : Questionnaires — PROMIS Physical Function v2.0: A validated, widely used patient-reported outcome measure assessing perceived physical capability to perform everyday activities. It provides standardized T-scores benchmarked to the general population and is sensitive to changes in postoperative recovery.
: Questionnaires — BREAST-Q Physical Well-Being: Chest and Abdomen Modules: Disease-specific instruments assessing physical comfort, function, and well-being at both the reconstructive (chest) and donor (abdominal) sites. These modules have been validated in autologous reconstruction populations and are sensitive to the functional and symptomatic aspects of DIEP flap recovery.
: Questionnaires — 3-Item Recovery Performance Index (RPI): A brief, low-burden instrument that asks patients to report their activity, energy, and overall functional level relative to their individual preoperative baseline. This simple metric complements wearable-derived data by capturing the patient's subjective perception of recovery in these three relevant domains.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07631026

SUMMARY:

## NCT07636980
Empowered Relief for Caregivers (ER-CY): A Co-Designed Brief Intervention to Support Families of Youth With Chronic Pain

Intervention: : Empowered Relief for Caregivers (ER-CY) — Empowered Relief for Caregivers is a single-session, skills-based behavioral intervention delivered virtually via Zoom in one session of approximately two hours. Co-designed with caregivers, it adapts the Empowered Relief approach for caregivers of youth with chronic pain, teaching strategies to reduce caregiver distress and to respond more adaptively to a child's pain. All participants receive the same single class; there are no additional sessions or booster components.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07636980

SUMMARY:

## NCT07640945
Efficacy and Neural Mechanisms of MRI-Guided Accelerated Continuous Theta Burst Stimulation Targeting the Inferior Parietal Lobule in Generalized Anxiety Disorder: A Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : continuous theta burst stimulation(cTBS) — Accelerated cTBS treatment was administered over 5 days, five times daily (1800 pulses per session, a total of 9000 pulses per day, with 50-minute intervals between sessions), at a stimulation intensity of 80%-120% of the resting motor threshold. The precise stimulation group targeted locations based on individual baseline functional MRI data, calculating the coordinates of strongest functional connectivity between the right IPS and the right basolateral amygdala (BLA), and implemented via neuronavigation system registration; the non-precise stimulation group targeted the cortical projection coordinates of the P4 electrode site according to the international 10-20 EEG system; the sham stimulation group used the same parameters but with a sham coil.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07640945

SUMMARY:

## NCT07729059
Effects of an Individualized, Smartwatch-Supported Exercise Intervention on Physical Activity, Cardiorespiratory Fitness, Limb Function and Quality of Life in Adolescent and Young Adult Survivors of Bone and Soft Tissue Sarcoma: A Randomized Controlled Trial (Sport Ist Herzenssache)

Intervention: : Individualized Exercise Program — An 8-week individualized, partially supervised home-based exercise program designed to increase physical activity in accordance with exercise guidelines for cancer survivors. The program is tailored to each participant's functional status, based on a comprehensive baseline assessment (functional testing, cardiopulmonary evaluation, and a structured counseling session addressing individual interests, concerns, and goals).

The program consists of:

Endurance training: 60 minutes/week (e.g., walking, cycling), Aerobic and strength training: 3 sessions/week, 30 minutes each, targeting endurance, strength, coordination, mobility, balance, and flexibility

Training sessions are self-administered at home, supported by a digital training platform providing instructional videos, and monitored via a wearable activity tracker. Partial supervision is provided through live video-conferencing sessions conducted by a trained exercise therapist in weeks 1, 2, 3, 5, and 7.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07729059

SUMMARY:

## NCT07748767
Association of Micro-Nanoplastic Exposure With Coronary Microvascular Dysfunction and Subsequent Cardiovascular Outcomes: A Prospective Cohort Study

Intervention: : micro-nanoplastic examination — In this prospective cohort study, the investigators plan to perform a low-contamination protocol for the detection of circulating micro-nanoplastic particles in eligible subjects prior to the administration of invasive coronary angiography and concomitant coronary microvascular function assessment, so as to clarify the impact of micro-nanoplastic exposure status on coronary microvascular function and long-term prognosis.
: Microvascular Function Examination — Using a catheter to perform invasive coronary functional assessment, including flow fraction reserve(FFR), coronary flow reserve (CFR) and index of microcirculation resistance (IMR)

## NCT07775040
A Prospective, Single-arm, Multicenter Phase II Clinical Study to Evaluate the Efficacy and Safety of Spatially Fractionated Radiotherapy Combined With Tislelizumab and Platinum-based Doublet Chemotherapy as Induction/Conversion Therapy for Potentially Resectable Stage III Non-small Cell Lung Cancer With Bulky Disease.

Intervention: : Spatially Fractionated Radiotherapy (SFRT) — Participants receive lattice radiation therapy to the primary lung tumor. The gross tumor volume (GTV) is delineated, and lattice target volumes (GTV-Lattice) are generated using a hexagonal close-packed model within the tumor. Volumetric modulated arc therapy (VMAT) plans are designed. Prescription dose: GTV receives 20 Gy in 5 fractions, and GTV-Lattice receives 60 Gy in 5 fractions. After plan verification, treatment is delivered on 3 non-consecutive working days (e.g., Monday, Wednesday, Friday).
: Tislelizumab — 200 mg administered via intravenous infusion on Day 1 of each 21-day cycle (Q3W) for 2 to 4 cycles, starting on Day 8-15 following SFRT.
: Carboplatin — AUC 5 administered via intravenous infusion on Day 1 of each 21-day cycle (Q3W) for 2 to 4 cycles. Used in combination with either pemetrexed (for non-squamous histology) or paclitaxel (for squamous histology), at the investigator's discretion.
: Cisplatin — 75 mg/m² administered via intravenous infusion on Day 1 of each 21-day cycle (Q3W) for 2 to 4 cycles. Used in combination with either pemetrexed (for non-squamous histology) or paclitaxel (for squamous histology), at the investigator's discretion. Adequate hydration and antiemetic prophylaxis are required.
: Pemetrexed — 500 mg/m² administered via intravenous infusion on Day 1 of each 21-day cycle (Q3W) for 2 to 4 cycles. Indicated only for patients with non-squamous NSCLC. Vitamin B12 and folic acid supplementation are required per standard practice.
: Paclitaxel — 175 mg/m² administered via intravenous infusion on Day 1 of each 21-day cycle (Q3W) for 2 to 4 cycles. Indicated only for patients with squamous NSCLC. Premedication to prevent hypersensitivity is required per standard practice.
: Radical Lung Resection Surgery — Definitive radical surgery is performed 4 to 6 weeks (±7 days) after the last dose of neoadjuvant therapy. Surgical approaches include minimally invasive techniques (video-assisted thoracoscopic surgery \[VATS\] or robotic-assisted surgery) or open thoracotomy. Procedures include lobectomy, bilobectomy, pneumonectomy, or sleeve resection, combined with ipsilateral systematic mediastinal lymph node dissection.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07775040

SUMMARY:

## NCT07785544
Efficacy and Safety of Low-Dose Duloxetine Plus Alpha-Lipoic Acid Versus Standard-Dose Duloxetine in Painful Diabetic Peripheral Neuropathy: An Assessor-Blinded Randomized Controlled Non-Inferiority Trial

## NCT07834658
Effects of Transcutaneous Tibial and Auricular Vagus Nerve Stimulation on Pain and Autonomic Function in Females With Primary Dysmenorrhea

Intervention: : Transcutaneous Tibial Nerve Stimulation (TTNS) — Electrical stimulation will be delivered through a TENS device using surface electrodes placed over the posterior tibial nerve for 30 mins according to the study protocol for the management of primary dysmenorrhea. The intervention will be applied for 5 consecutive days, including 2 days before and 3 days during the mensturation period.
: Transcutaneous Auricular Vagus Nerve Stimulation (TaVNS) — Electrical stimulation delivered through a TENS device using surface electrodes placed on the auricular branch of the vagus nerve for 30 mins according to the study protocol for the management of primary dysmenorrhea. The intervention will be applied for 5 consecutive days, including 2 days before and 3 days during the mensturation period.
: TTNS+TaVNS — Electrical stimulation delivered through a TENS device to both the posterior tibial nerve and the auricular branch of the vagus nerve sequentially according to the study protocol. The intervention will be applied for 5 consecutive days, including 2 days before and 3 days during the mensturation period.
: Sham Stimulation — Sham stimulation delivered using the same TENS device and electrode placement as the active intervention but without therapeutic electrical stimulation. The intervention will be applied for 5 consecutive days, including 2 days before and 3 days during the mensturation period.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07834658

## NCT07873177
A Pilot Randomized Controlled Trial: Internet-Delivered Cognitive Behavioural Therapy Program for Menopausal Vasomotor Symptoms

Intervention: : Internet-delivered Cognitive Behavioural Therapy — This study is a randomized waitlist-controlled pilot study. The ICBT-Meno-VS program is based on CBT-Meno, a structured CBT protocol and accompanying workbook developed to help individuals manage menopause-related symptoms, including vasomotor symptoms, mood, sleep, and anxiety (Green et al., 2012; Green et al., 2019). The ICBT-Meno-VS program adapts these CBT-based strategies for delivery through an online, therapist-guided format and focuses on managing menopause-related vasomotor symptoms. Eligible participants will be randomly assigned to receive the 4-week ICBT-Meno-VS program immediately or to a 4-week waitlist. Participants assigned to the intervention group will complete the 4-week ICBT-Meno-VS program, followed by a post-treatment assessment, and a 3-month follow-up assessment. Participants assigned to the waitlist will complete a 4-week waitlist period and assessment, after which they will receive the 4-week ICBT-Meno-VS program, followed by a post-treatment assessment.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07873177

SUMMARY:

## NCT07914010
Reducing Exposure With Air Cleaners and Technology (REACT) in At-Risk Communities

Intervention: : Air Cleaner — During Phase 2 of the study, an air cleaner will be installed in each participant's household. All participants will receive this device and intervention.
: PM2.5 Monitor with Visual Air Quality Indicator — To assess how visual input from a low-cost particulate matter monitor can increase the use and effectiveness of air cleaners, participants will be randomized to receive monitors with a visual indoor air quality indicator (Arm 1, intervention) and without a visual indoor air quality indicator (Arm 2, control). Participants will be randomized 1:1 using blocks into the intervention arm or control arm.
: Educational Materials — All participants will receive educational materials about the PurpleAir monitor, air cleaner and its best use, and the Air Quality Index. For those in Arm 1, the second half of the Air Quality Index fact sheet will describe the visual air quality indicator on their PurpleAir monitor. For those in Arm 2, the second half of the fact sheet will share general information about where local air quality information can be found.
: Air Cleaner Technical Assistance — To evaluate how additional information and/or engagement can further impact air cleaner use and effectiveness, investigators will provide additional technical assistance on air cleaner usage in three different forms: (1) text messages that reinforce air cleaner use through reminders and tips and targets spatial barriers and in-the-moment behaviors; (2) short-form videos that show the participants how to use their air cleaner and share community member perspectives on benefits, safety concerns, and household dynamics; and (3) video calls with study staff to build skills and confidence and reinforce value of using the air cleaner. Participants will be randomized to receive one, two, or all three of these forms of air cleaner technical assistance.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07914010

SUMMARY:

## NCT07947342
Chemotherapy-Minimized, Blank Microsphere-Based Transarterial Chemoembolization (BMS-TACE) in Patients With BCLC Stage B/C Hepatocellular Carcinoma Resistant to FOLFOX-Based Hepatic Arterial Infusion Chemotherapy: A Single-Center, Single-Arm, Phase 2 Trial

Intervention: : BMS-TACE — Visceral angiography of the celiac trunk, superior mesenteric artery and phrenic arteries maps the arterial supply of the tumor. A 2.8F microcatheter is placed super-selectively into each tumor-feeding artery by coaxial technique. A minimal-dose chemoemulsion (low-dose pirarubicin in a small volume of lipiodol) is injected first. Blank microspheres of any manufacturer are then delivered in ascending size order: 40-120 microns mixed with 5 mL contrast to occlude the distal tumor arterial bed; 100-300 microns for tumor arterial branches, with supplementary lipiodol emulsion if vascular lakes are seen; then 300-500 or 500-700 microns for the main feeding trunk. The endpoint is contrast stasis over 3-5 cardiac cycles with loss of at least 90% of intrahepatic tumor staining on completion angiography. Imaging is repeated every 6 weeks; retreatment follows protocol-defined criteria. Performed by one of two designated interventional radiologists.
: Pirarubicin — Low-dose pirarubicin emulsified with a small volume of lipiodol and injected intra-arterially into the tumor-feeding artery immediately before blank microsphere embolization, as the minimal-dose chemotherapy adjunct of each BMS-TACE session.
: Blank microspheres — Calibrated blank (non-drug-loaded) microspheres, 40-120, 100-300, 300-500 and 500-700 microns, of any manufacturer, delivered in ascending size order as the principal embolic agent in BMS-TACE.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07947342

SUMMARY:

## NCT07947550
Diagnostic Accuracy of Combined Perfusion Index, Internal Jugular Vein Collapsibility Index and Cardiac Index in Predicting Post-Spinal Hypotension During Elective Cesarean Section: A Prospective Randomized Study

Intervention: : pulse oximeter to measure Perfusion Index (PI) — is measured using a validated multi-parameter monitor with a pulse oximetry module (Mindray uMEC 12 or equivalent).

* The probe is placed on the right index finger in the supine position before induction of spinal anesthesia block.
* Three consecutive readings are recorded at 1-minute intervals.
* These steps will be repeated with another reading after delivery of baby.
* Then the mean value is used as the baseline PI.
* The cut-off applied is: -
* 3.5 or less (low risk for hypotension)
* Greater than 3.5 (high risk for hypotension)
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07947550

## NCT07948421
Effectiveness of Customized Oral Hygiene Instructions on Periodontal Health in Patients Undergoing Fixed Orthodontic Treatment With Poor Plaque Control: A Single-Blinded Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Customized Oral Hygiene Instructions — Individualized oral hygiene education delivered by an oral health professional in a 10-15-minute session. Participants receive verbal instructions, a leaflet, and hands-on toothbrushing and interdental cleaning training directly in their own mouth. Plaque-disclosing dye guides instruction toward areas with plaque accumulation. Instructions are reinforced at orthodontic visits. Toothbrushes and interdental brushes are provided at baseline and 6 months.
: Conventional Oral Hygiene Instructions — Conventional oral hygiene education delivered by an oral health professional. Participants receive verbal instructions, a leaflet, and demonstrations of toothbrushing and interdental cleaning around orthodontic brackets using a dental model. Toothbrushes and interdental brushes are provided at baseline and 6 months.

## NCT07948694
Implementing Multiple Process Refinements for Optimal Value in Endo-vascular Treatment: A Cluster Randomized Clinical Trial

Intervention: : Multicomponent Quality Improvement Intervention — This intervention consists of a structured, multicomponent quality improvement program named TIPS, designed to optimize endovascular treatment (EVT) workflow in acute ischemic stroke. TIPS stands for Training \& Discussion, Indicator Feedback, Professional Guidance, and Solutions Toolkit. The program includes standardized training sessions, real-time performance feedback via an electronic data capture (EDC) system, 24/7 expert consultation, and process management tools such as the iStroke platform and GreenFast pathway. The intervention is delivered at the hospital level and implemented using a Plan-Do-Study-Act (PDSA) cycle. It is distinct from other QI initiatives by its structured SPO-based design (Structure-Process-Outcome) and its integration of real-time data monitoring and expert-guided iterative feedback.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07948694

SUMMARY:

## NCT07948915
Sexual Function Outcomes Following Peri-clitoral PRP Added to Anterior Vaginal Wall PRP: A Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Platelet-Rich Plasma (PRP) Injections — A single treatment session. 40-60 mL of autologous whole blood is processed with the Arthrex Angel system (15-minute centrifugation), yielding approximately 2-4 mL of leukocyte-rich platelet-rich plasma (PRP), which is used within 2 hours of preparation. After topical anesthesia, all participants receive two-thirds of the PRP volume injected superficially into the distal anterior vaginal wall, approximately 3 cm from the urethral meatus, using a 28-32 gauge needle.

Experimental arm: The remaining PRP (approximately 1 mL) is delivered as two superficial injections of about 0.5 mL each near the junction of the clitoris, clitoral hood, and frenulum.

Sham comparator arm: A needle is inserted at the same peri-clitoral sites without injecting any substance.

Participants are blinded to peri-clitoral assignment.

Discoid/Double Discoid Excision Versus Segmental Resection for Rectal Deep Endometriosis Nodules <5 cm: A Randomized Controlled Trial of Bowel Complications and Functional Outcomes

Intervention: : Laparoscopic Discoid or Double Discoid Excision — Laparoscopic mobilization of the rectum followed by full-thickness excision of the endometriosis nodule using a circular stapler. Sequential double discoid excision using two circular staplers will be performed when required by nodule size. An intraoperative air leak test will assess staple-line integrity. Pelvic drainage and a diverting stoma will be used selectively according to protocolized indications.
: Laparoscopic Segmental Rectal Resection — Laparoscopic mobilization and resection of the affected bowel segment, followed by a stapled end-to-end or end-to-side colorectal anastomosis, with mesorectal preservation close to the bowel wall. An intraoperative air leak test will assess anastomotic integrity. Pelvic drainage and a diverting stoma will be used selectively according to protocolized indications.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07949136

SUMMARY:

## NCT07949292
Effects of Home Visits and Telephone-Supported Educational Interventions on Diabetes Knowledge, Self-Care Behaviors, and Diabetes-Related Difficulties in Individuals With Type 2 Diabetes Attending a Family Health Center

Intervention: : Nurse-Led Home Visit-Based Diabetes Education — A nurse will provide four structured, face-to-face diabetes education sessions in participants' homes, once weekly for four weeks. Each visit will last approximately 45-60 minutes and use PowerPoint presentations, informational brochures, direct instruction, and questions and answers. Weekly topics will cover: (1) diabetes, symptoms, risk factors, complications, hypoglycemia, hyperglycemia, and regular health checks; (2) nutrition, carbohydrate management, food label reading, portion control, and physical activity; (3) medication adherence, insulin use, blood glucose monitoring, foot care, oral care, sleep, and screening; and (4) diabetes-related difficulties, stress management, problem-solving, social support, personal strengths, goal setting, and behavior change.
: Nurse-Led Telephone-Supported Diabetes Education — A nurse will provide four structured tele-nursing sessions over one month, once weekly, using telephone calls, video calls, and digital educational materials, including PowerPoint presentations and brochures. Each session will last approximately 15-25 minutes, with additional counseling time when needed. The educational content will be similar to that provided to the home visit group. Weekly topics will cover: (1) diabetes, symptoms, risk factors, complications, hypoglycemia, hyperglycemia, and regular health checks; (2) nutrition, carbohydrate management, food label reading, portion control, and physical activity; (3) medication adherence, insulin use, blood glucose monitoring, foot care, oral care, sleep, and screening; and (4) diabetes-related difficulties, stress management, problem-solving, social support, personal strengths, goal setting, and behavior change. Sessions will reinforce learning, support motivation, and address individual self-care needs.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07949292

SUMMARY:

## NCT07949773
Operational and Clinical Evaluation of the Treatment Planning Software and Oncology Information System for Pediatric Proton Therapy

Intervention: : RayTracker and RayAdaptive Software Analysis — Part of a not yet FDA-cleared software, the RaySearch treatment planning system and RayCare oncology information system. RayTracker enables longitudinal evaluation of treatment by recalculating dose on daily images and accumulating dose across fractions using deformable image registration, thereby providing an estimate of the cumulative delivered dose to targets and organs at risk. RayAdaptive uses daily treatment imaging (such as cone-beam CT (CBCT)) together with the planning CT to generate an updated representation of patient anatomy and recalculation of dose on the current patient anatomy, allowing clinicians to evaluate whether anatomical changes could meaningfully alter the planned dose distribution. These features are designed to be used in tandem. Their combined capabilities are intended to support clinical decision-making related to treatment monitoring and potential adaptation.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07949773

SUMMARY:

## NCT07949903
Follow-up of Unruptured Intracranial Aneurysms With Ultra-High Field 7 Tesla MRI

Intervention: : 3T and 7T UHF MRI — After obtaining the signed informed consent and inclusion in the study, demographics will be assessed. The timing of the 3T + 7T UHF MRI examination is not determined by the date of enrolment but by the timing of the baseline examination and must be no later than 12±1 months after that.

The exam will take place at the Translational Imaging center in Sitem-insel. Before the MRI examination, participants are asked to provide information on risk factors for UIA and medication history via a questionnaire. For the MRI examination, we will use a 3 T scanner with a standard head coil provided by the the MR-vendor and a 7 T whole-body MRI scanner equipped with a 1-channel transmit and 32-channel receive head coil. The 7T protocoll will comprose arterial ToF angiography and vessel wall imaging sequences. Within 4-8 weeks after the 3T + 7T UHF MRI examination, a chart review of the booard decision and outpatient visits will be performed to determine whether management changed from observation.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07949903

## NCT07950943
Effect of Displaying Predicted Consequences of AI Treatment Recommendations on Clinicians' Fluid and Vasopressor Decisions in Sepsis: A Randomized Crossover Study

Intervention: : Case review with on-duty plan (B0) — Participants review the case together with the on-duty clinician's actual intravenous fluid rate and vasopressor dose and submit a final plan. No AI information is shown. This condition controls for the effect of a second review.
: AI recommendation (B1) — As B0, plus the AI output: either a recommended dose level for intravenous fluid and for vasopressor, shown with the corresponding dose range, or a statement that the evidence is insufficient to recommend a change.
: AI recommendation with predicted consequences (B2) — As B1, with identical AI output, plus the model's predicted mean arterial pressure, lactate and urine output over the next 4 hours under the on-duty plan and under the recommended plan, shown as point estimates.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07950943

## NCT07950982
Ultrasound-Guided Subtransverse Interligamentary (STIL) Block Versus Paravertebral Block (PVB) for Postoperative Analgesia in Modified Radical Mastectomy: A Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Ultrasound-Guided Subtransverse Interligamentary (STIL) Block — Ultrasound-guided Subtransverse Interligamentary (STIL) block performed at the T4 thoracic level approximately 30 minutes before induction of general anesthesia. A 22-gauge echogenic spinal needle will be advanced in-plane beneath the intertransverse ligament into the subtransverse interligamentary space. Following negative aspiration, 25 mL of 0.25% bupivacaine combined with dexamethasone 4 mg will be injected incrementally.
: Ultrasound-Guided Thoracic Paravertebral Block (PVB) — Ultrasound-guided thoracic paravertebral block performed at the T4 thoracic level approximately 30 minutes before induction of general anesthesia. A 22-gauge echogenic spinal needle will be advanced in-plane toward the thoracic paravertebral space. Following negative aspiration, 25 mL of 0.25% bupivacaine combined with dexamethasone 4 mg will be injected incrementally.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07950982

SUMMARY:

## NCT07951021
Chemotherapy-Minimized, Blank Microsphere-Based Transarterial Chemoembolization (BMS-TACE) Combined With Low-Dose Lenvatinib, Iparomlimab and Tuvonralimab, and Stereotactic Body Radiotherapy in Patients With BCLC Stage C Hepatocellular Carcinoma and Macrovascular Tumor Thrombus: A Single-Center, Single-Arm, Phase 2 Trial

Intervention: : BMS-TACE — Visceral angiography of the celiac trunk, superior mesenteric artery and, as indicated, collateral arteries maps the arterial supply of the tumor and tumor thrombus and identifies arteriovenous shunting. A 2.8F microcatheter is placed super-selectively into tumor-feeding arteries by coaxial technique. A fixed, minimal-dose chemoemulsion (doxorubicin 10 mg and lobaplatin 10 mg in about 5 mL lipiodol) is injected slowly; chemotherapy may be omitted in later sessions according to tumor response, leaving blank-microsphere embolization alone. Blank microspheres are then delivered in ascending size order (40-120, 100-300, then 300-500 or 500-700 microns). The endpoint is contrast stasis over 3-5 cardiac cycles plus angiography 5-10 minutes later confirming loss of more than 90% of tumor staining. Imaging is repeated 4-6 weeks after each session; retreatment follows protocol-defined criteria. Participants generally receive two complete sessions.
: Lenvatinib — Lenvatinib 8 mg orally once daily for body weight 60 kg or above, or 4 mg orally once daily for body weight below 60 kg, started 3 to 7 days after the first BMS-TACE. Lenvatinib is withheld on the day of each BMS-TACE or SBRT and resumed 3 to 5 days afterwards provided the Child-Pugh score is 5 to 8; cumulative interruption does not exceed 3 weeks. Treatment continues until disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, or withdrawal of consent.
: Iparomlimab and Tuvonralimab — Iparomlimab and tuvonralimab (QL1706), an anti-PD-1/anti-CTLA-4 bispecific antibody combination, 5 mg/kg intravenously every 3 weeks, infused over at least 30 minutes. Dosing starts 3 to 7 days after the first BMS-TACE and is separated from any BMS-TACE by at least 3 days. Treatment continues until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity. Immune-related adverse events are managed according to protocol-defined rules, including dose interruption, corticosteroid therapy and permanent discontinuation criteria.
: Stereotactic Body Radiotherapy — Stereotactic body radiotherapy directed primarily at the macrovascular tumor thrombus. Simulation uses vacuum-bag or thermoplastic immobilization with 4D-CT or gated CT (slice thickness 3 mm or less). The prescription is 30 to 50 Gy in 5 to 10 fractions, individualized by tumor size and location, normal liver volume and Child-Pugh score: 40-50 Gy in 5 fractions when normal liver volume is 700 mL or more with Child-Pugh 5-6; 35-40 Gy in 5-8 fractions when normal liver volume is 500-700 mL or Child-Pugh is 7; and 30-40 Gy in 10 fractions when the target is within 1 cm of stomach, duodenum or colon. The prescription covers at least 95% of the planning target volume. Daily kV cone-beam CT image guidance is used with setup correction to within 3 mm. A minimum interval of 14 days separates SBRT and BMS-TACE in either direction.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07951021

SUMMARY:

## NCT06885684
Developing Cognitive Control and Metacognition to Reduce the Functional Impact of Restricted and Repetitive Behaviors in Autism

Intervention: : Cognitive Control Training + Metacognition Coaching — Computer training is delivered in person by a coach. Training consists of four games; each requires multiple aspects of cognitive control. Training levels progress in difficulty by increasing the number of items or simultaneous dimensions, decreasing the available response time, requiring greater accuracy, or adjusting the proportion of distractors or their complexity. Parameters that influence the difficulty of each level and passing criteria are fixed, but children progress from level to level at their own pace. Training is accompanied by manualized metacognition coaching to provide strategies to meaningfully engage with the tasks, simplify the tasks, support sustained effort, emphasize key cognitive control skills needed, provide coping strategies to reduce frustration associated with challenging aspects of the games, and use guided conversations to highlight metacognition.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06885684

SUMMARY:

## NCT07356479
Enhancing Mental and Physical Health of Women Through Engagement and Retention (EMPOWER) 3.0 (QUE 25-015)

Intervention: : Evidence-Based Quality Improvement Booster (EBQI/B) — EBQI is a systematic quality improvement method for engaging frontline practices in improvement that introduces "best science" and evidence in the service of operational goals. EBQI has been tested in several VA implementation trials and the VA Office of Women's Health (OWH) recently conducted a large-scale roll out of EBQI across VA women's health. Sites randomized to EBQI/B will receive a two-hour EBQI Booster delivered virtually to site-identified QI champions and local implementation teams for each EBP reviewing key EBQI components and providing examples grounded in the EBPs.
: Evidence-Based Quality Improvement Booster + External Facilitation (EBQI/B+EF) — EBQI is a systematic quality improvement method for engaging frontline practices in improvement that introduces "best science" and evidence in the service of operational goals. EBQI has been tested in several VA implementation trials and the VA Office of Women's Health (OWH) recently conducted a large-scale roll out of EBQI across VA women's health. External facilitation (EF) delivered by an expert facilitator from outside of the local implementation site, offers a supportive coaching process in which facilitators encourage interactive problem-solving in improvement efforts. EF is one of the most-studied implementation strategies. Sites randomized to EBQI/B+EF will receive a two-hour EBQI Booster delivered virtually to site-identified QI champions and local implementation teams for each EBP reviewing key EBQI components and providing examples grounded in the EBPs, plus 12 months of EF.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07356479

SUMMARY:

## NCT07463638
Effect of Mindfulness Intervention Based on Virtual Reality Technology on Anxiety and Pain in Minimally Invasive Vascular Surgery Under Local Anesthesia: a Single-center, Prospective, Randomized Controlled Clinical Study

Intervention: : VR-Based Mindfulness Intervention — The VR-based mindfulness intervention will be delivered in two stages on the day of surgery. Before surgery, participants in the intervention group will be transferred to a separate room within the preoperative waiting area, where a mindfulness-trained nurse will assist them in using Huawei VR Glass to receive prerecorded mindfulness education and guided mindfulness practice. At the start of surgery, participants will receive an intraoperative VR-based mindfulness intervention using prerecorded guided mindfulness content, assisted by a trained nurse. Following the completion of the first intraoperative VR-based mindfulness session and the corresponding anxiety assessment, the intervention will be resumed and may be repeated according to the duration of surgery until the end of surgery.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07463638

## NCT07509203
ScvO2-Centered Optimisation of RED Blood Cell Transfusions (SCORED) - Protocol for a Multicenter, Randomised, Controlled Clinical Trial to Compare the Central Venous Saturation (ScvO2) Guided Transfusion Trigger Versus Transfusion Based on Clinician's Decision in the Critically Ill

Intervention: : Measurement of central venous saturation — After randomisation central venous saturation will be measured. If the central venous saturation is below 70%, 1 unit of red blood cells will be transfused. The central venous saturation will be measured post transfusion, and if it is still below 70%, further 1 unit of RBCs will be transfused. Measurements and transfusions continue until ScvO2 reaches 70% or hemoglobin level increases above 9g/dl.

If ScvO2 is above 70%, no transfusion will be administered. ScvO2 will be measured again between 4-12 hours after the initial measurement. If it is still above 70%, no transfusion will be administered, and no further measurement will be performed on the given day.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07509203

## NCT07567508
Early Detection of Concealed Cardiac Amyloidosis Using AI-ECG and CT-Derived Extracellular Volume in Patients With Atrial Fibrillation

Intervention: : AI-ECG analysis — Artificial Intelligence-enhanced Electrocardiogram (AI-ECG), developed by Mayo Clinic, is gaining attention as a non-invasive screening tool. AI-ECG predicts myocardial amyloid deposition based on a standard 12-lead ECG with high accuracy, AUC 0.84(95% CI 0.82-0.86) In particular, it demonstrated superior performance with an AUC of 0.9 or higher in ECGs exhibiting low voltage or pseudo-infarction patterns, suggesting its potential to detect the disease even before structural changes become apparent.
: CT-ECV analysis — Myocardial Extracellular Volume (CT-ECV) analysis using cardiac CT is a tissue characterization technique that can be easily added to conventional cardiac CT protocols, enabling the quantification of myocardial fibrosis or infiltrative diseases. Among 874 subjects who underwent coronary CT, 12.4% exhibited a CT-ECV of ≥ 35%, and cardiac amyloidosis was incidentally discovered in 14.3% of these individuals.
: Atrial fibrillation treatment — Standard care and treatment in accordance with established AF guidelines.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07567508

SUMMARY:

## NCT07580690
Integrating Health and Social Service Needs for Frail Elders at Point of Care: Development and Evaluation of an IT-based Digital Needs-assessment Tool

Intervention: : Core: Digitalised SST-informed discharge-planning pathway — Trained patient navigators complete the digital Simple Segmentation Tool (SST) during the hospital stay: a six-category Global Impression rating and 12 Complicating Factors. A rule-based algorithm maps these ratings to health and health-related social service needs and to locally available services. The recommendations may be tailored to patient preferences, family context, existing support and clinical judgement, and inform the discharge care plan. For services the participant accepts, an Agency for Integrated Care (AIC) care coordinator facilitates the referral, including assessment of eligibility for subsidised services where required.
: Structured post-discharge follow-up calls — Two structured calls from an AIC care coordinator at 14 (+/-7) and 75 (+/-7) days after discharge, in addition to routine practice. In each call the coordinator reviews progress with referred services, explores reasons for declining services, identifies barriers to starting or continuing services, liaises with service providers as needed, and gives feedback to the clinical team so that plans can be modified where indicated.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07580690

## NCT07635589
Bridging the Gap Between Exercise Oncology Research and Its Implementation in Clinical Practice

Intervention: : Exercise — PRE-TREATMENT: Frequency: 2-3 supervised exercise sessions/week (45-60 min/session) with 30 minutes of walking on non-supervised days. Intensity: Rating of Perceived Exertion (RPE) 5-7/10. Type: Multimodal (aerobic + resistance training). Time: 3 to 12 weeks of supervised exercise.

ON TREATMENT: Frequency: 2 supervised exercise sessions/week (45-60 min/session) with 30 minutes of walking on non-supervised days. Intensity: RPE 3-5/10. Type: Multimodal (aerobic + resistance training). Time: 12 weeks of supervised exercise.

POST-TREATMENT: Frequency: 3 supervised exercise sessions/week (45-60 min/session) for the first 4 weeks; 2 supervised + 1 independent exercise sessions per week for the next 4 weeks; 1 supervised + 2 independent exercise session for the next 4 weeks; 3 independent exercise sessions for the final 4 weeks + 30 minutes of walking on non-supervised days. Intensity: RPE 5-7/10. Type: Multimodal (aerobic + resistance training). Time: 16 weeks tapered exercise program.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07635589

## NCT07658079
The Effect of the Distance of the Percutaneous Endoscopic Gastrostomy (PEG) Feeding Tube From the Pylorus on Postoperative Outcomes

Intervention: : Percutaneous endoscopic gastrostomy — The exact placement of the PEG feeding tube, both on the skin and within the gastric lumen, is not fully understood. In this study, researchers aimed to measure the distance of the feeding tube from the pylorus in patients undergoing PEG surgery, investigate how this affects post-procedure outcomes, and determine the optimal level within the gastric lumen. Additionally, the catheter placement on the skin will be recorded for each patient. The anatomical skin localization obtained by analyzing data from all patients will be defined as the 'Çaykara Triangle'.

## NCT07788599
Renforcement, Orientation, Santé, Empowerment

Intervention: : Self-sampling for six sexually transmitted infections — Participants will complete a questionnaire, with the assistance of the investigator, collecting sociodemographic information and information related to their sex work activity. They will then be offered a pre-labelled self-sampling kit containing: 1- a three-site sampling test for chlamydia and gonorrhoea; 2- a dried blood spot (DBS) card for self-collection of blood samples which will be analysed for HIV, hepatitis B, hepatitis C and syphilis. Participants may decline the kit. They will perform the sampling independently at home or at a place of their choice. Samples will be returned by post to the virology / bacteriology laboratories or handed to the ROSELA team during a physical outreach activity. Pseudonymised results will be made available to the CeGIDD of Montpellier and communicated to participants according to their preferred method. In case of a positive result, appropriate medical care, including further investigations and treatment, where required, will be offered.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07788599

## NCT07879196
Two Sessions of Coach-Delivered Core Transformation for Psychological Distress and Well-Being in Community Adults: A Randomized Waitlist-Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Core Transformation Process — Core Transformation (CT) is a structured, manualized process. A trained coach guides the person, step by step, from an issue they choose (a feeling, behavior or thought they want to change) to a strong, positive state of being, called a "Core State" in CT. The coach then guides them to bring that state back to the original issue. Each participant has two one-to-one sessions of about 90 minutes over Zoom, about one week apart. Coaches follow a manualized protocol. Sessions are given by CT coaches in training (Cohort 1) or certified CT coaches (Cohort 2), and are video-recorded.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07879196

SUMMARY:

## NCT07898345
Prognostic Value of Functional-Nutritional Status in Patients Aged 65 Years and Older With Acute Coronary Syndrome

Intervention: : Functional-Nutritional Burden — Observed prognostic exposure, not an assigned intervention. Functional-nutritional burden is the number of impaired domains: (1) probable pre-frailty or frailty before the acute event (Age Is Not a Barrier score of 3 or more, or Clinical Frailty Scale score of 4 or more); (2) malnutrition according to the GLIM criteria assessed in all participants at inclusion. Unknown domain status is not treated as absent. Risk of malnutrition by MNA-SF (11 points or less) is recorded separately; research blood samples (albumin, high-sensitivity C-reactive protein) are analyzed in exploratory analyses.

## NCT07944300
Magnetic Resonance Cholangiopancreatography for Predicting Difficult ERCP Cannulation in Patients With Concomitant Cholelithiasis and Choledocholithiasis Undergoing Laparoendoscopic Rendezvous

Intervention: : Magnetic resonance cholangiopancreatography (MRCP) with structured blinded morphometry — Routine preoperative MRCP within 14 days before surgery on a 1.5 T scanner (3 T recorded as a covariate) after a fast of at least 6 hours, with 200 mL of pineapple or blueberry juice as negative oral contrast and no gadolinium. Minimum protocol: axial and coronal T2 HASTE/SSFSE, axial T1 in-phase and opposed-phase, coronal thick-slab RARE MRCP at 15° radial increments, respiratory-triggered navigator-gated 3D T2 turbo spin-echo MRCP (near-isotropic 1.0-1.4 mm voxels), diffusion-weighted imaging and thin-section axial T2 through the second part of the duodenum. Two radiologists, blinded to the outcome and to each other, measure the pre-specified terminal biliary geometry, periampullary duodenum, and ductal caliber and stone variables on curved planar, oblique-coronal and axial reformats using a locked structured form. Measurements are withheld from the operating team until the cannulation phase has ended.
: Single-stage laparoendoscopic rendezvous with a standardized, capped cannulation phase — Performed for clinical indication under general anesthesia by consultant hepatobiliary surgeons. Laparoscopic cholecystectomy to a photographed critical view of safety; cystic ductotomy, 5 Fr transcystic catheter and intraoperative cholangiography, with the guidewire held proximal to the terminal duct. With the duodenoscope in the short position, wire-guided cannulation of the native papilla is attempted until biliary access or until an ESGE threshold is reached (5 minutes of cumulative cannulation time, a sixth attempt required, or a second unintended pancreatic duct cannulation); the transcystic guidewire is then advanced antegrade across the papilla and captured. Precut, transpancreatic sphincterotomy, double-guidewire and pancreatic stent techniques are not used before the rendezvous wire. Duct clearance, completion cholangiography and closure follow. Rectal indomethacin or diclofenac 100 mg is given at induction unless contraindicated.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07944300

SUMMARY:

## NCT07946380
Mesopancreas Excision Versus Standard Dissection During Pancreaticoduodenectomy for Resectable Pancreatic Ductal Adenocarcinoma: A Single-Center Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Pancreaticoduodenectomy with total mesopancreas excision — Open pancreaticoduodenectomy (pylorus-preserving or pylorus-resecting) with an artery-first approach. The mesopancreas is excised en bloc with the pancreatic head and uncinate process within these boundaries: the superior mesenteric vein/portal vein axis and the right semicircumference of the superior mesenteric artery anteriorly and medially; the adventitia of the inferior vena cava, left renal vein and aorta posteriorly; the origins of the celiac trunk and superior mesenteric artery superiorly; and the lower border of the uncinate process inferiorly. The artery is cleared in the periadventitial plane over about 180 degrees on its right side from the aorta to the first jejunal artery, with ligation of the inferior pancreaticoduodenal artery at its origin; the left-sided nerve plexus is preserved. Extension up to 270 degrees is allowed only for suspected periarterial tumor extension. ISGPS standard lymphadenectomy.
: Standard pancreaticoduodenectomy — Open pancreaticoduodenectomy (pylorus-preserving or pylorus-resecting) using the conventional vein-first approach: Kocher maneuver, tunneling behind the pancreatic neck and neck transection, then division of the uncinate process from the superior mesenteric vein and from the right side of the superior mesenteric artery, with ligation of the pancreaticoduodenal branches close to the pancreas. The artery is not dissected in the periadventitial plane, its nerve plexus is preserved, and the retropancreatic tissue is not excised as an en bloc compartment. ISGPS standard lymphadenectomy (stations 5, 6, 8a, 12b1, 12b2, 12c, 13a, 13b, 14a, 14b, 17a and 17b), with station 14a and 14b nodes removed superficial to the nerve plexus.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07946380

SUMMARY:

## NCT07946393
Correlation of Preoperative Serum CA 19-9 With the Pattern and Timing of Recurrence After Pancreatoduodenectomy for Pancreatic Ductal Adenocarcinoma: A Retrospective Cohort Study

Intervention: : Pancreatoduodenectomy — Curative-intent pancreatoduodenectomy (classical Whipple or pylorus-preserving; open or minimally invasive) with standard ISGPS lymphadenectomy, with mesopancreatic excision, an artery-first approach and venous resection where indicated, performed as routine care at the Liver and GIT Hospital, Minia University, between 1 January 2017 and 31 December 2024. Specimens were reported synoptically against the AJCC eighth edition dataset, applying the 1 mm margin rule. The operation is not assigned, scheduled or modified by the study; operative and pathology data are extracted retrospectively from existing records.
: Index preoperative serum CA 19-9 — Exposure of interest. Serum CA 19-9 measured during routine preoperative care by electrochemiluminescence immunoassay and retrieved from laboratory records; no sample is taken for the study. Index value: the last CA 19-9 within 30 days before operation with a contemporaneous total bilirubin ≤2 mg/dL (≤34 µmol/L) drawn within 72 hours of the CA 19-9 sample; after preoperative biliary drainage, the post-drainage value meeting this criterion (pre-drainage values are recorded but never used). Where several values qualify, the one closest to surgery is used. Analysed as binary (\>37 vs ≤37 U/mL; primary contrast), ordinal (\<2, 2-37, \>37-200, \>200 U/mL) and continuous (log2\[CA 19-9 + 1\]) variables.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07946393

SUMMARY:

Risk Factors and Early Predictors of Bile Leak After Major Hepatectomy: A Prospective Observational Cohort Study

## NCT07952594
Immersive and Personalized Virtual Reality for Women's Well-Being During Childbirth: Proof of Concept and Feasibility Study With Women in the Prodromal Phase and the First Stage of Labor

Intervention: : Personalized Immersive Virtual Reality Session — Participants will complete a single session using a head-mounted immersive virtual reality device. The intervention consists of exposure to personalized virtual environments, including realistic natural scenarios and customizable visual and auditory stimuli selected according to individual preferences. Sounds may include nature sounds, music, or audio tracks guiding diaphragmatic breathing or relaxation techniques; visual elements may include people, objects, or animals. The environment is personalized by the operator through a smartphone interface connected to the headset, which also allows real-time monitoring of what the participant is viewing. Before the session, participants receive a brief familiarization period with the device. The virtual reality exposure lasts approximately 20 minutes, or less according to participant tolerance, and is followed by assessments of usability, acceptability, tolerability, emotional state, sense of presence, engagement, and possible side effects.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07952594

## NCT07952880
Clinical Validation of the Aptitude Medical Systems Metrix CT/NG/TV Test for Detection of Chlamydia Trachomatis (CT), Neisseria Gonorrhoeae (NG), and Trichomonas Vaginalis (TV) in Self-Collected Vaginal Swab Specimens

Intervention: : Metrix CT/NG/TV Test — The Metrix CT/NG/TV Test is a real-time reverse transcription loop-mediated isothermal amplification reaction (RT-LAMP) test that has been designed to detect DNA from Chlamydia trachomatis, Neisseria gonorrhoeae, and Trichomonas vaginalis via electrochemical detection using the Metrix Reader. Detection of Chlamydia trachomatis, Neisseria gonorrhoeae, and Trichomonas vaginalis gene targets will produce a positive result, differentiated by each target. If no target is detected and the internal control amplifies, the result is negative. If no amplification is detected in any of the channels, an invalid result is displayed.

## NCT07952971
Effects of Pilates Versus Brügger's Exercises on Pain, Craniovertebral Angle, and Functional Impairment in Patients With Upper Crossed Syndrome

Intervention: : Pilates Exercise — Pilates Exercise are Structured into 4 Weeks Progressive Traning Blocks. The Session lasts for 45 min with 3 sessions per week. Every active exercise will be performed 3 sets of 10 repetitions and in stretching 10-30 sec hold will be added.

Inensity Progresses from Standard PT protocol in 1st week to Neuromuscular Activation Exercises in 2nd and 3rd Week and Strengthening of Cervical and Scapular Muscles in 4rd week
: Brügger's Exercise — Brügger's Exercise are Structured into 4 Weeks Postural Correction Traning. The Session lasts for 45 min with 3 sessions per week.

During Week 1 Standard PT protocol is followed, in Week 2 unweighted Brügger's exercises performed for 15 repetitions over 1 to 2 sets with an initial 10-second hold that increases by 5 seconds every two days.In Week 3 resistance is added using elastic band , maintaining the 10-second hold time but increasing the volume to 2 to 3 sets of 15 reps. By Week 3, the heavy enduranceis added , requiring 3 full sets of 15 repetitions with the hold time doubled to 30 seconds.
: Standard Physical Therapy — Hot packs were applied to patients for a period of 10 minutes. Cervical Isometrics with 20 sec hold and 5reps/3 sets Stretching of Cervical as well as Pect muscles were Performed with 20 sec hold and 5reps/2 sets Scapular Mobility Excerises

Assessing the Impact of an Over-the-Counter Continuous Glucose Monitor in Primary Care to Improve Metabolic Health for Adults of South Asian or Filipino Ancestry in California: LINGO Study

Intervention: : Over-the-Counter Continuous Glucose Monitor (OTC CGM) — Participants will wear an unblinded over-the-counter continuous glucose monitor continuously for 10 weeks. The device provides real-time glucose measurements and glycemic trend information, allowing participants to observe the effects of dietary choices, physical activity, and daily behaviors on glucose levels.
: Lifestyle Education Program — Participants will receive a structured lifestyle education program designed to support healthy behavior change. Educational content will focus on nutrition, physical activity, weight management, and strategies for improving glycemic control. The program will be delivered over a 10-week period and is anticipated to include eight educational sessions. The sessions are intended to help participants interpret glucose patterns and apply evidence-based lifestyle modifications to improve health outcomes.
: Usual Care — Participants will continue to receive standard clinical care for management of prediabetes or type 2 diabetes as determined by their usual healthcare providers. No additional study-specific glucose monitoring or lifestyle education interventions will be provided beyond routine clinical management.

## NCT07953205
A Phase I, Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled, Single-Center, Single and Multiple Ascending Intravenous Infusion Study to Evaluate the Safety, Tolerability, Pharmacokinetics, and Immunogenicity of VDJ006 for Injection in Healthy Subjects

Intervention: : Recombinant human interleukin-1RN-Fc fusion protein (VDJ006) — VDJ006 is an injectable, long-acting recombinant human interleukin-1 receptor antagonist (IL-1RN) fused to the Fc region of human IgG, expressed in Chinese hamster ovary (CHO) cells. It binds tightly to the interleukin-1 receptor type I (IL-1R1), blocking IL-1α and IL-1β from engaging their receptor and thereby antagonizing IL-1-mediated inflammatory signaling. Compared with wild-type IL-1RN-Fc, VDJ006 shows substantially enhanced affinity for IL-1R1 across species (e.g., \~13.4-fold for human IL-1R1), supporting its development as a novel, extended-half-life IL-1 pathway inhibitor for cryopyrin-associated periodic syndromes (CAPS) and other IL-1-driven inflammatory diseases.
: Placebo — This is a matching placebo formulation containing no active pharmaceutical ingredient (API). The placebo is manufactured to be identical in appearance, color, shape, size, and packaging to the investigational product VDJ006 to maintain the integrity of the double-blind design. It is utilized solely as a control intervention for the purpose of this randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled Phase I clinical trial
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07953205

## NCT07953335
Comparative Effects of Mackenzie and Mulligan Mobilization With Suboccipital Inhibition on Pain, Disability, and Range of Motion in Cervicogenic Headache

Intervention: : Active Comparator: McKenzie Method + suboccipital muscle inhibition — A received standard physiotherapy and suboccipital muscle inhibition, followed by repeated cervical movements based on directional preference. The protocol included cervical retraction, retraction with extension, retraction with side flexion, retraction with rotation, cervical flexion, supine cervical retraction and supine cervical extension. Selected movements were performed for 2 sets of 10 repetitions, with each repetition held for 2-5 seconds. Exercises were supervised by the therapist and were guided by symptom response. Upper trapezius, levator scapulae and sternocleidomastoid stretches were performed for 3 repetitions with a 30-second hold
: Experimental: Mulligan Mobilization (SNAGs) + McKenzie Method (MDT) +Suboccipital Inhibition (SMI). — Group B received the same standard physiotherapy, suboccipital inhibition and McKenzie-based exercises as Group A. C1-C2 rotational SNAGs were additionally applied in a pain-free manner. The therapist applied a unilateral posterior-anterior glide to the transverse process of C1 on the side opposite the restriction identified during the FRT. Participants actively rotated towards the restricted side as far as possible without pain while the glide was sustained. Three sets of 6 repetitions were performed during each session. The McKenzie movements were performed for 2 sets of 10 repetitions with 2-5-second holds, and the same stretching dosage was used in both groups

## NCT07953881
A Phase II, Single-Arm, Multicenter Study Evaluating the Prognosis of Patients With Non-Small Cell Lung Cancer Achieving Major Pathological Response and Negative Circulating Tumor DNA After Neoadjuvant Immunotherapy Without Adjuvant Treatment

Intervention: : Omission of Postoperative Adjuvant Therapy — Patients with resectable non-small cell lung cancer (NSCLC) who achieve a major pathological response (MPR) following neoadjuvant immunotherapy plus chemotherapy and have negative circulating tumor DNA (ctDNA) 21-30 days after complete surgical resection will not receive postoperative adjuvant anticancer therapy.

Participants will undergo scheduled postoperative surveillance, including chest computed tomography (CT) and ctDNA testing every three months during the first year and every six months during the second year. Additional imaging assessments will be performed according to the study protocol.

If ctDNA becomes positive during follow-up, repeat ctDNA testing and imaging assessments will be conducted. Further management will be determined based on clinical findings and multidisciplinary evaluation.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07953881

SUMMARY:

## NCT07953894
Effects of Seven Stones Game Versus Champ Ball Skills Program on Object Control Skills and Eye-Hand Coordination in School Going Children

Intervention: : Seven Stone Game — Participants in the Seven Stones Game group will receive a structured and supervised Seven Stones Game intervention three times per week for 8 weeks. Each session will include a 7-minute warm-up, 5-minute skill priming, approximately 20-22 minutes of Seven Stones Game activities, and a 5-minute cool-down. The intervention will progressively develop throwing, catching, aiming, hand-eye coordination, bilateral coordination, reaction, and decision-making skills. The activities will be progressed across four phases over the 8-week intervention period, with increasing task difficulty, variability, and use of different throwing distances, ball sizes, target sizes, and hand-use requirements.
: Champ Ball Skills Program — Participants in the Champ Ball Skills Program group will receive a structured and supervised ball skills intervention three times per week for 8 weeks. Each session will include a 7-minute warm-up, 8-minute skill instruction, 20-minute drill-based practice, and a 5-minute cool-down. The program will focus on throwing, catching, aiming, ball handling, eye-hand coordination, reaction, and decision-making through progressively challenging drills. The intervention will progress from basic throwing and catching activities to overhand throwing, one-handed and alternate-hand catching, moving-target activities, reaction-based tasks, non-dominant hand activities, and precision and decision-making drills.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07953894

## NCT07954258
Epigenetic Modifications Following Topical Estradiol Treatment for Skin Rejuvenation in Peri- and Post-Menopausal Women: A Pilot Study

Intervention: : Topical Estradiol — The Principal Investigator assigns each participant to an arm based on menopausal status and systemic HRT use: (1) no systemic HRT + topical estradiol 0.01% (n=40: 20 peri-, 20 post-menopausal); (2) systemic HRT + topical estradiol 0.01% (post-menopausal, n=20); (3) systemic HRT + topical estradiol 0.01% + topical rapalog serum (post-menopausal, n=20); (4) no systemic HRT + placebo (vehicle) cream (n=20: 10 peri-, 10 post-menopausal). Participants in Arms 2-3 must have been on continuous systemic estrogen-containing HRT for at least 12 weeks and must not start, stop or change the dose during the study; participants in Arms 1 and 4 must not start systemic HRT.

Study cream is applied to the face once nightly for 12 weeks, followed by a 4-week wash-out with no test product (16 weeks in total). All participants use a standard cleanser (Neutrogena Ultra Gentle Daily Cleanser) and sunscreen (Neutrogena Ultra Gentle Daily Facial Moisturizer SPF 30) daily.

## NCT07954297
"Toujours Dimanche": A Nature-Based Mindfulness and Self-Compassion Meditation Program Delivered Through Immersive Virtual Reality During Hemodialysis. A Pilot Randomized Controlled Feasibility Trial

Intervention: : Nature-Based Virtual Reality Mindfulness and Self-Compassion Program — A structured mindfulness and self-compassion program delivered through a Meta Quest 3 immersive virtual reality headset using 360-degree videos of Quebec landscapes and natural soundscapes. Participants receive 20-minute sessions twice weekly for 16 weeks during hemodialysis. Each session consists of 10 minutes of guided meditation followed by 10 minutes of free exploration of the virtual environment. A repeating four-week cycle addresses sensory awareness, awareness of bodily sensations, self-compassion and gratitude, and a peaceful relationship with oneself and nature. A trained research assistant helps participants operate the headset and remains available during sessions.
: Mental Health Educational Brochure — An educational brochure entitled "Prendre soin de sa santé mentale en hémodialyse" (Taking Care of Your Mental Health During Hemodialysis) is provided as the educational comparator. Participants receive the brochure without the virtual reality mindfulness and self-compassion program during the 16-week intervention period.

## NCT05977556
The Effect of Perioperative Prehabilitation on Markers of Fitness and Frailty in Patients Undergoing Elective Surgery: a Pilot, Pragmatic, Randomized Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Combined neuromuscular exercise training and 'sit less, move more' program — Neuromuscular strength training: The experimental group will receive up to 24 sessions of neuromuscular strength training led by a physical therapist with expertise in geriatrics and movement behaviors. The program includes exercises such as pelvic lifts, lunges, step-ups, squats, and sit-stands. The difficulty level will progressively increase using techniques like manual resistance, dumbbells, and unstable surfaces.

Sedentary behavior intervention: The experimental group will also undergo a sedentary behavior intervention based on social cognitive theory. It involves weekly coaching sessions to enhance self-efficacy in reducing sedentary time and increasing light-intensity activity throughout the day. Participants will be provided with a wrist-worn Fitbit activity monitor for daily feedback and to track adherence to the intervention. The aim is to encourage participants to "sit less and move more" throughout their waking hours.

## NCT06166290
Ultrasonic Aspiration (Sonopet) vs Electrosurgical Fulguration for the Surgical Management of Perianal Condyloma: a Randomized Controlled Trial.

Intervention: : Ultrasonic aspiration (Sonopet) — Patient positioning will be done, and the patient will be anesthetized with local infiltration of 2% lidocaine, a nerve block, or general anesthesia. The surgical field will be disinfected and draped. The perianal and anal area will be investigated for lesions. The Sonopet device, operating at two selectable frequencies-25 kHz, a universal setting for general tissue fragmentation and emulsification, and 34 kHz, specifically designed for delicate tissue removal in superficial lesions-will be used to precisely target and remove warts. Any necrotic tissue will be aspirated, and residual debris will be wiped away with a piece of moist gauze. Bacitracin ointment will be applied over the treated areas. The procedure will take approximately 15-20 minutes. Upon discharge, acetaminophen 1000 mg every 8 hours and ibuprofen 600 mg every 8 hours will be prescribed for pain.
: Electrosurgical Fulguration — The procedure is described as follows. Patient will be administered regional or general anesthesia, placed in prone jack-knife position. Prepping and draping will be done. The perianal and anal area will be investigated for lesion. The lesions will be excised, and the bases will be fulgurated. Electrosurgery device will be used in monopolar setting (30 Cut mode, 30 Coag mode). After procedure local analgesics total MERCAINE/EPI 0.25% 20 ml on both sides of the lesion. Bacitracin cream applied over the treated areas. Upon discharge 1000 mg of Tylenol every 8 hours along with 600 mg of ibuprofen every 8 hours.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06166290

## NCT06528353
SCREEN-HPV: A Study Evaluating a Blood Biomarker-based Screening for HPV-driven Oropharyngeal Carcinoma

Intervention: : HPV16-E6 serology and HPV ctDNA — For participants seropositive for HPV16-E6, regular clinical and biological follow-up visit will be scheduled until lesion identification. In the event of a malignant lesion identified on clinical examination and/or imaging, treatment will be performed according to the standard of care. In the absence of any suspicious lesion, but in the event of HPV ctDNA positivity, prophylactic surgery of the tonsils and base of the tongue (the vast majority of HPV-related OPC develop in these regions) will be proposed to the patient, given the high risk of OPC. In case of refusal of this prophylactic surgery, close clinical and imaging monitoring will be performed.

Surgical specimens will be analyzed to confirm or not the presence of HPV-driven OPC and HPV ctDNA will be measured post-operatively to ensure disease clearance.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06528353

SUMMARY:

## NCT06663202
Fibre-Retention Osseous Resective Surgery (FibReORS) vs Conservative Surgery With or Without Papilla Preservation Technique for the Treatment of Residual Pockets: a 5-year Randomized Clinical Trial

Intervention: : Fiber retention osseous resective surgery — At the buccal and lingual sides, paramarginal or intrasulcular incisions will be made based on probing pocket depth values and the width of keratinised tissues.

Thinned palatal flap technique will be performed on the palatal side. Bone remodelling will be carefully made using manual or rotary instruments in order to reshape positive attached fibres/bony architecture utilising the FibReORS technique, great attention will be made to distinguish between inflammatory granulation tissue and connective tissue fibres attached to root cementum by means of periodontal probe. Flaps will be positioned at the level of the alveolar crest without tension.
: Papilla preservation flap — Surgical access will be obtained using modified papilla preservation techniques and when interproximal space is narrow (less than 3mm in width) incision will be made using the simplified papilla preservation flap. Both flaps buccal and lingual/palatal will be elevated in any case to allow root surface cleaning and defects debridement under direct vision. Minimally invasive flap elevation preventing to damage inter proximal tissues with the preservation of the papilla. Defects debridement with mini-curettes. Root surface debridement with ultrasonic debridement with periotip and mini-curettes. Flaps will be positioned at the pre-surgical level without any tension. Monofilament non-resorbable 5-0 e-PTFE suturing material will be used. Suturing will be performed with external vertical mattress sutures to achieve primary intention closure.
: Widman flap procedure — Surgical access will be obtained using the modified Widman flap technique. Intrasulcular incision will be done on both buccal and lingual/palatal sides without attempting to preserve the interdental tissues and interdental tissues will be removed. Both flaps buccal and lingual/palatal will be elevated at full thickness in any case to allow root surface cleaning and defects debridement under direct vision, a vertical releasing incision could be made when needed.

Defects debridement with mini-curettes. Root surface debridement with ultrasonic debridement with periotip and mini-curettes. Flaps will be positioned at the level of the alveolar crest without tension. Monofilament nonresorbable 5 - 0 e/PTFE suturing material will be used. Suturing will be performed with single interrupted sutures.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06663202

SUMMARY:

## NCT06786247
Evaluation of an Asynchronous Remote Communities Approach to Behavioral Activation for Depressed Adolescents

Intervention: : ActivaTeen — The ActivaTeen app is built on HIPAA-compliant instance of a popular messaging software. Participants in the treatment group can access the app at any time via desktop or mobile, where they can find features designed to enhance their receipt of BA therapy.

These features include: weekly BA skills and homework completion support, in-app messaging with their BA therapist, mood and activity tracking and data visualizations, chatbot check-ins on progress and guided problem-solving around barriers, and moderated peer support using the ActivaTeen app. The app will have a moderator, who is part of the study team, to manage peer-to-peer interactions within ActivaTeen.
: Behavioral Activation — Behavioral activation therapy involves individual weekly sessions with a trained therapist for 12 weeks. The sessions are completed either in-person or via telehealth. The treatment focuses on behavioral manifestations of depression such as isolating, and how these behaviors are interconnected with low mood and depressive emotions. BA helps patients find ways to engage in behaviors that are meaningful to them based on their values, priorities, and goals, with the aim of creating positive loops between doing meaningful activities and improved mood. The therapist provides structure and problem-solving for activating these behaviors and keeping track of changes over time.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT06786247

## NCT06801431
A Pilot Study on the Tolerance, Feasibility, and Impact of Transcutaneous Spinal Cord Stimulation on Bowel Management in Individuals With Motor Complete Spinal Cord Injury Receiving Inpatient Care

Intervention: : Transcutaneous spinal cord stimulation — Individuals will receive a 10-day course of daily, 30-minute sessions of transcutaneous spinal cord stimulation to the thoracolumbar spine cord using the Chattanooga Mobile 2 Stim Device. These sessions will be conducted in the morning before bowel care and therapy. Cathode pads will be placed between the T10 and T11 spinous processes and the T12 and L1 spinous processes. Anode pads will be placed over the iliac crests. Stimulation will consist of 1 millisecond duration, biphasic pulses, at 30 Hertz frequency. The intensity of the current, will be adjusted to be suprasensory and sub-motor, and no more than 140 milli-amperes.

## NCT07269509
Evaluation of the Efficacy and Safety of an Intensified Strategy of Intratonsillar Immunotherapy (ITIT) for Allergic Rhinitis: A Multicenter, Randomized, Double-blind, Controlled Trial

Intervention: : Basic Intratonsillar Immunotherapy (ITIT) — During injection, the operator needs to gently shake the injection bottle about 20 times, the drug must be mixed to ensure the consistency of allergen concentration, and carefully check the patient's name and concentration on the bottle. Allergy extracts should not be injected intravenously, so the syringe will be aspirated to avoid inadvertent intravascular injection. Before each injection, after inserting the needle into the selected tonsil, before injecting the dose, the investigator will slightly pull the plunger of the syringe. If blood returns from the syringe, the syringe, and its contents will be discarded. The other tonsil will be selected and a new syringe will be prepared.

Inject the mite allergen solution into the tonsil at months 0, 1, 2, 12, and 24, and administer the placebo at months 6 and 18.
: Strengthen Intratonsillar Immunotherapy (ITIT) — During injection, the operator needs to gently shake the injection bottle about 20 times, the drug must be mixed to ensure the consistency of allergen concentration, and carefully check the patient's name and concentration on the bottle. Allergy extracts should not be injected intravenously, so the syringe will be aspirated to avoid inadvertent intravascular injection. Before each injection, after inserting the needle into the selected tonsil, before injecting the dose, the investigator will slightly pull the plunger of the syringe. If blood returns from the syringe, the syringe, and its contents will be discarded. The other tonsil will be selected and a new syringe will be prepared.

Inject mite allergen solution into the tonsil at the 0th, 1st, 2nd, 6th, 12th, 18th and 24th months.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07269509

## NCT07522190
Randomized Controlled Trial of Center-Based Early Start Denver Model (ESDM) vs. Pivotal Response Treatment (PRT) in Children With Autism

Intervention: : Early Start Denver Model (ESDM) — ESDM is a comprehensive, play-based early intervention that integrates applied behavior analysis with developmental and social-pragmatic approaches. ESDM uses a structured curriculum to systematically target communication, social engagement, imitation, cognition, and play skills. The intervention is delivered within joint activity routines that emphasize the interactions between the child and adult. Goals are individualized and embedded into naturalistic play and daily routines to support developmental progress. Children will participate in center-based treatment 4 days per week for 3 hours per day for 24 weeks. The parent will be encouraged to practice the intervention at home and submit one 10-minute parent-child interaction video regularly during the 24 weeks.
: Pivotal Response Treatment (PRT) — PRT s a naturalistic, play-based intervention based on principles of applied behavior analysis. Instead of working on developing skills in isolation, PRT focuses on "pivotal" areas of development like motivation, responsivity to cues, and self-initiation. The idea is that working on these areas will lead to broad improvements across communication and behavior. Through the intervention, therapists and parents integrate various strategies into child-led play to increase communication and engagement. Children will participate in center-based treatment 4 days per week for 3 hours per day for 24 weeks. The parent will be encouraged to practice the intervention at home and submit one 10-minute parent-child interaction video regularly during the 24 weeks.
: Developmental Reciprocity Treatment (DRT) — DRT is a relationship-based, developmental intervention that targets the foundations of social reciprocity such as shared attention, emotional engagement, and back-and-forth interaction. DRT emphasizes building the child's capacity for mutual engagement with parents or therapists through interactions that follow the child's lead. This will be a 24-week home-based intervention for with weekly parent training
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07522190

SUMMARY:

## NCT07616167
From Stroke Rehabilitation to Independence: An Impairment-Aware Control Framework for Adaptive Exoskeleton Assistance

Intervention: : Unified Control Framework for Lower-Limb Powered Orthosis — An AI-driven, machine learning-based control software integrated into a wearable lower-limb powered orthosis. The system utilizes a Bayesian Neural Network (BNN) to analyze a user's pathological walking patterns (kinematics) in real-time via onboard sensors. Based on this real-time performance, the device dynamically modulates its physical assistance along a seamless continuum. It automatically transitions between stiff corrective guidance (position-based gait training) when the user struggles, and compliant, volitional torque support (torque-based assistance) as the user's independent walking ability improves.
: Conventional Robotic Controller — A standard control paradigm for lower-limb powered orthoses that provides non-adaptive physical assistance during gait training. Depending on the trial block, the device operates in one of two static modalities: either rigid position-based gait training (GT) that physically guides the patient's limbs through a fixed, predetermined trajectory regardless of effort, or torque-based volitional augmentation (VA) that proportionally amplifies existing muscle output or ground reaction forces. Unlike the experimental intervention, this controller cannot interpret kinematics in real-time or dynamically modulate assistance along a continuous spectrum based on the user's instantaneous performance.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07616167

SUMMARY:

## NCT07677930
Prevent Worsening of Depressive Symptoms Using Digital Cognitive Behavioral Therapy for Insomnia and Circadian Intervention in College Students

Intervention: : digital cognitive behavioral therapy for insomnia and digital circadian intervention — For the combined dCBT-I + dCI program, additional circadian intervention will be included to support entrainment of the biological clock. These components include circadian education, strategies to gradually advance circadian phase, advice on morning sunlight exposure, and components of social rhythm therapy to establish a consistent daily sleep routine (e.g., consistent meal times and exercise).

The digital intervention will be implemented through a therapist-guided using motivational interviewing approach. Participants will be motivated to advance their sleep schedule by following fixed daytime activities using motivational interviewing techniques to support their autonomy and self-efficacy in making behavioral changes
: digital cognitive behavioral therapy for insomnia — Digital CBT-I has been developed by our group based on a well-established framework that includes sleep education, behavioral strategies such as stimulus control and sleep restriction, constructive worry strategies, cognitive restructuring, relaxation training and sleep hygiene education. For the dCBT-I alone group, therapists will primarily review participants' sleep schedule, setting sleep restriction window, and problem-solving efforts.
: digital health education — Participants in the control group will be app-based general health education with an equivalent dosage as the two intervention groups. To control for therapist contact time, the health education control group will also receive brief telephone follow-up sessions that focus solely on reviewing the content of the educational modules, without incorporating any active sleep-related intervention strategies.
Link: https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/NCT07677930
